# Round 35 of the relation-map review

Read the round-32 brief for the scope, guarantees and approval bar: `audit_evidence_claims/m32/brief.md`. Read the round-34 brief and report: `audit_evidence_claims/m34/brief.md` and `audit_evidence_claims/m34/REPORT.md`. The repairs are in `CODEX_MAP_M1_VERDICT.md`, section "Round 34".

Review the latest commit on HEAD. Run the tests with `python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider`; expect 363 passing. Start the page with `python src/app.py <port>`, using any port other than 8765.

## What changed since round 34

- **M34-B1.** In `src/app.py` `Engine.__init__`, both name tables now take every stored spelling of a drug: each drug's preferred name and every entry in its alias list, plus the lookup index. The refusal check in `explore` therefore sees spellings the index dropped. One consequence: a preferred name with the same `_sig` as a refused spelling is refused too. `CABOZANTINIB S-MALATE` is refused because the resolver blocks `Cabozantinib (s)-malate`. This is recorded as an over-refusal.
- **M34-S2.** `_sig` keeps optical-rotation descriptors. `(+/-)` and `(±)` become `±`, `(-)` becomes `−` (U+2212), and `(+)` keeps its `+`.
- **M34-S1.** In `src/query.py`, the preferred-name rule for a shared key is blocked only by a closing bracket before the name or an opening bracket after it. Brackets around the name no longer block it.

## What to do

1. Re-run M34-B1, S1 and S2, and the round-33 and round-32 findings. Mark each FIXED or NOT FIXED, with exact inputs.
2. Re-run the historical replays against the map, including the expanded source universe: every alias in `dictionary.drugs[*].aliases`, the index, labels and keys.
3. Attack the design as in the round-34 brief.
4. Check the question-linker change for regressions.
5. Briefly re-check guarantees A to G.
6. Report data problems separately from code problems.

Not blockers, already recorded:
- Leaks in the question boxes' free text, and the resolver's own lists (README defect 15).
- The over-refusals listed in `CODEX_MAP_M1_VERDICT.md`.
- Synonyms and brands the map does not draw.
- Two tracer drugs, and preferred names whose letters equal a refused spelling.
- THEOPHYLLINE's upstream drug type.

Write scratch files only under `audit_evidence_claims/m35/`. Put the report in `audit_evidence_claims/m35/REPORT.md`. Do not edit tracked files. End with exactly one line: `VERDICT: APPROVE` or `VERDICT: CHANGES REQUIRED`.

+# Independent relation-map review — round 35
+
+Reviewed **HEAD `56eb3d6297f2878d008e5edd9eb5eade62a0841c`**, parent `1f6017e8ae7923d21cfafca64b1285331e0d2c18`, on 2026-11-04. **CHANGES REQUIRED: one code blocker.** The exact M34-B1, S1 and S2 reproductions are fixed, but the optical-sign repair runs before removal of invisible characters. An invisible character inside `(-)` makes its minus disappear, admitting a negative optical isomer as an unqualified preferred-name node. +## Code blocker
+
+### M35-B1 — an ignored invisible character defeats optical-sign preservation
+
+**Location:** `src/app.py:278-283`, `_sig`. The new regular expressions recognize optical descriptors before the final character filter removes invisible characters and combining marks. An inserted U+200B is not matched by `\s*`; consequently the ASCII minus remains punctuation and is deleted by that final filter. There is no later descriptor pass.
+
+The following are fresh HTTP results. In escaped spellings, `\u200b`, `\ufeff`, `\u2060`, and `\u0301` denote **actual single Unicode characters**, not six literal backslash characters. Complete inputs, responses, source records, and code-point captures are linked below.
+
+| Exact input / reproducible Python spelling | `_sig` | Map result |
+|---|---|---|
+| `ARTEMISININ` | `artemisinin` | center **CHEMBL269671** |
+| `(-)-ARTEMISININ` | `−artemisinin` (U+2212) | `not_one_name` |
+| `"(-\u200b)-ARTEMISININ"` | `artemisinin` | **center CHEMBL269671** |
+| `"(-\ufeff)-ARTEMISININ"` | `artemisinin` | **same center** |
+| `"(-\u2060)-ARTEMISININ"` | `artemisinin` | **same center** |
+| `"(-\u0301)-ARTEMISININ"` | `artemisinin` | **same center** |
+| `"(\u2212\u200b)-ARTEMISININ"` | `−artemisinin` | `not_one_name` |
+| `(+)-ARTEMISININ` | `+artemisinin` | `alias_only` |
+| `CHEMBL269671` | ID path | center CHEMBL269671, permitted ID control |
+
+[Independent root HTTP/source confirmation](root_optical_confirmation.json), [separate HTTP/code-point reproduction](attacks/rotation_http.json), [70-case descriptor mutation matrix](attacks/rotation_mutations.json), [source pointers and summary](attacks/rotation_source_and_summary.json). The matrix has **56 sign-normalization invariance failures and 14 wrong-sign ARTEMISININ draws**; these are variants of one defect, not 14 independent blockers.
+
+**Identity evidence:** `data/processed/resolved.json:1`, `dictionary.drugs.CHEMBL269671`, has preferred name `ARTEMISININ`, with `aliases[1].alias == "(+)-artemisinin"`; raw provenance is `data/raw/drugs.json:1`, `data[768].drug`, including its first ChEMBL synonym. The live node has the same preferred label. ChEBI identifies artemisinin as the positive optical form. A primary synthesis study explicitly distinguishes synthetic negative artemisinin from its natural positive antipode. [ChEBI](https://www.ebi.ac.uk/chebi/CHEBI:223316), [Krieger et al., 2018, PMID 29723442](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29723442/). The source record is consistent with those references; this is a **code normalization defect**, not a newly misfiled synonym.
+
+**Why this is blocking in this round:** the new repair explicitly makes the optical descriptor part of the name, while the retained comparison contract ignores invisible characters and accents. Those rules together require `(-)-ARTEMISININ` and `(-\u200b)-ARTEMISININ` to retain the same negative sign. The latter instead draws a different optical form. The exact original M34-S2 examples are fixed; that does not close this composition failure. Round 34 treated punctuation erasure as a nonblocking limitation of the then-prescribed comparison. This round explicitly changes that prescription to preserve optical descriptors. This finding therefore does not silently broaden the review to arbitrary chemical punctuation.
+
+Under the **buggy implementation** of `_sig`, the admitted input does equal the stored signature. An oracle that calls that same function consequently passes. The counterexample rests on the two documented transformation requirements and independently established identity, rather than a claimed failure of that circular membership test. It is not one of the recorded over-refusals, excluded brands/synonyms, tracer exceptions, or free-text question leaks.
+
+The repair needs to make ignored-character/diacritic normalization and optical-descriptor recognition compose correctly before punctuation is discarded, or fail closed when an optical descriptor cannot be interpreted safely. Verification should preserve `−`, `+`, and `±` through invisible/combining-character variants and must not enable additional stored-name admission. No production repair was made.
+

+## Question-linker regression, separate from the blocker
+
+### M35-S1 — wrapping the preferred name hides an adjacent stereochemical descriptor
+
+**Nonblocking question-only regression**, `src/query.py:898-899`. The new boundary expression permits an opening bracket immediately before the preferred-name token and a closing bracket immediately after it. It does not inspect past those brackets for a descriptor. Exact parent/current comparisons show:
+
+| Input | Parent query.py | HEAD query.py / HTTP |
+|---|---|---|
+| `What trials evaluate (+/-)(NICOTINE)?` | no entity/evidence | CHEMBL3 NICOTINE, two evidence rows |
+| `What does (+/-)[NICOTINE] target?` | no entity/evidence | CHEMBL3 NICOTINE, two evidence rows |
+| `What trials evaluate (NICOTINE)(+/-)?` | no entity/evidence | CHEMBL3 NICOTINE, two evidence rows |
+| `What trials evaluate [NICOTINE](+/-)?` | no entity/evidence | CHEMBL3 NICOTINE, two evidence rows |
+| `What trials evaluate (NICOTINE)(+)?` | no entity/evidence | CHEMBL3 NICOTINE, two evidence rows |
+
+The bare racemic control `(+/-)NICOTINE` names CHEMBL440464. All three question APIs receive the erroneous CHEMBL3 evidence for these wrapped cases. The map returns `not_one_name`, so this does **not** independently fail the map approval bar. [Expanded parent/current comparison](contract/expanded_question_comparison.json), [summary and HTTP cases](contract/expanded_question_summary.json), [root corroboration](root_exact_http.json).
+
+The expanded comparison covers **six shared preferred names × 26 formats × two question wrappers = 312 cases**, with 110 changed readings (a mixture of intended recall recoveries and descriptor regressions), **zero map centers**. The inherited **99-key / 708-case** comparison and all **36 shipped demos** are unchanged. Therefore the repair note's corpus result is reproduced, but that older corpus misses the new bracket-descriptor composition. [Shared-key corpus](contract/shared_key_question_comparison.json), [demos](contract/demo_previous_comparison.json). The separate 47-product/141-question refusal matrix retains its expected refusals. [Product controls](contract/product_questions.json).

+import json, pathlib, sys, urllib.request, unicodedata
+
+ROOT=pathlib.Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]
+OUT=ROOT/'audit_evidence_claims/m35'
+sys.path.insert(0,str(ROOT/'src'))
+import app
+e=app.Engine()
+cases={
+ 'M34-B1':['Cabozantinib (s)-malate','cabozantinibsmalate','CABOZANTINIB S-MALATE','Cabozantinib (s)-malate?','C a b o z a n t i n i b   ( s ) - m a l a t e','Cabozantinib/(s)-malate','Cabozantinib\u200b(s)-malate','Cabozantinib (s)-malate™','CHEMBL2103868'],
+ 'M34-S2':['(+)-NICOTINE','(+)NICOTINE','(-)-nicotine','(+/-)NICOTINE','(±)-nicotine','NICOTINE','(−)-nicotine'],
+ 'optical-extra':['(-\u200b)-nicotine','(-\ufeff)-nicotine','(-\u2060)-nicotine','(\u200b-)-nicotine','(－)-nicotine','(–)-nicotine','(—)-nicotine','[-]-nicotine','(-\u200b)-epinephrine','(-)-epinephrine','(+\u200b/-)-nicotine','(+/−)-nicotine','(+−)-nicotine','(+–)-nicotine','(+)NICOTINE™'],
+ 'question-extra':['What trials evaluate (NICOTINE)?','What does (NICOTINE) target?','What trials evaluate [NICOTINE]?','What trials evaluate (+/-)NICOTINE?','What trials evaluate NICOTINE(+)?','What trials evaluate (+/-)(NICOTINE)?','What trials evaluate (+)(NICOTINE)?','What trials evaluate (-)(NICOTINE)?','What trials evaluate NICOTINE(+/-)?','What trials evaluate (NICOTINE)(+/-)?','What trials evaluate [NICOTINE](+/-)?','What trials evaluate (NICOTINE) (+/-)?','What trials evaluate NICOTINE and (+/-)NICOTINE?'],
+ 'data':['THEOPHYLLINE','Theophylline']
+}
+rows=[]
+for family, texts in cases.items():
+ for text in texts:
+  for route in (['/api/explore','/api/ask','/api/structured','/api/check'] if family=='question-extra' else ['/api/explore']):
+   payload={'text':text}
+   if route=='/api/check': payload['answer']='NICOTINE is evaluated in trials.'
+   if route=='/api/structured': payload['answer']='{"claims":[]}'
+   req=urllib.request.Request('http://127.0.0.1:8935'+route,data=json.dumps(payload).encode(),headers={'Content-Type':'application/json'})
+   with urllib.request.urlopen(req,timeout=180) as response: body=json.load(response); status=response.status
+   rows.append({'family':family,'input':text,'escaped':text.encode('unicode_escape').decode(),'codepoints':['U+%04X'%ord(c) for c in text],'sig':app._sig(text),'route':route,'status':status,'request':payload,'response':body,'linker':e.kg.link_entities(text)})
+  (OUT/'root_exact_http.json').write_text(json.dumps(rows,ensure_ascii=False,indent=2),encoding='utf-8')
+summary=[{'family':r['family'],'input':r['input'],'escaped':r['escaped'],'sig':r['sig'],'route':r['route'],'state':next((k for k in ['center','refused','choose','alias_only','not_one_name','not_found'] if k in r['response']),'question'),'id':r['response'].get('center',{}).get('id'),'entities':[(x['type'],x.get('id')) for x in r['linker']],'evidence_count':len(r['response'].get('evidence',[]))} for r in rows]
+(OUT/'root_exact_summary.json').write_text(json.dumps(summary,ensure_ascii=False,indent=2),encoding='utf-8')
+print(json.dumps({'requests':len(rows),'map_cases':[x for x in summary if x['family']=='optical-extra'],'question_cases':[x for x in summary if x['family']=='question-extra' and x['route']=='/api/ask']},ensure_ascii=False))

# Round 35 independent source and Spec review

Reviewed HEAD `56eb3d6297f2878d008e5edd9eb5eade62a0841c` against `1f6017e8ae7923d21cfafca64b1285331e0d2c18`. This lane writes only below `audit_evidence_claims/m35/attacks/`, runs with bytecode disabled, and uses the root-owned HTTP server on port 8935. No tracked or production-data file was edited.

## M35-B1: an ignored invisible inside a negative optical descriptor erases the descriptor

**Code blocker under the new optical-sign guarantee.** Exact input `(-)-ARTEMISININ` is undrawn (`not_one_name`), but `(-\u200b)-ARTEMISININ`, with an actual U+200B between the ASCII minus and closing parenthesis, draws **CHEMBL269671 ARTEMISININ**. Its signature is `artemisinin`, while the ordinary negative spelling has signature `−artemisinin` (U+2212). The same draw occurs with U+FEFF, U+200C, U+200D, U+2060, U+00AD or combining acute U+0301 in either position inside the descriptor. The U+2212 minus control stays significant and remains undrawn. [Exact HTTP inputs, code points and responses](rotation_http.json), [mutation matrix](rotation_mutations.json).

`src/app.py:278-279` recognizes the optical descriptor **before** `src/app.py:280-282` removes combining accents and invisible characters. U+200B is not `\s`, so the descriptor regex misses it. The subsequent filter drops both the invisible and the unconverted ASCII minus (punctuation). Thus the new rule's intended invariant, `_sig('(-\u200b)-ARTEMISININ') == _sig('(-)-ARTEMISININ')`, fails. This is a specific composition defect between two advertised rules: ignored invisibles/accents and retained optical signs. It is not a request to reject all punctuation-normalized names.

The source record is correct: `data/processed/resolved.json:1`, `dictionary.drugs.CHEMBL269671`, has preferred name `ARTEMISININ` and `aliases[1] = {alias: '(+)-artemisinin', type: 'synonym', source: 'ChEMBL'}`. The corresponding raw record is `data/raw/drugs.json:1`, `data[768].drug`. The live SQL node has the same preferred name. [Full exact records and source pointers](rotation_source_and_summary.json).

The identity distinction is independently supported: [ChEBI CHEBI:223316](https://www.ebi.ac.uk/chebi/CHEBI:223316) names the substance (+)-artemisinin and lists artemisinin as its INN; [Krieger et al., primary synthesis paper, PMID 29723442](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29723442/) explicitly distinguishes synthetic (-)-artemisinin from its natural (+) antipode. This supports a wrong stereochemical identity for the drawn negative-sign input; it does not establish a defective stored source record.

Round 34 treated the old `(+)-NICOTINE` to racemic-nicotine collision as nonblocking because the then-prescribed rule discarded punctuation indiscriminately. Round 35 explicitly changes that policy to preserve optical descriptors, and `_sig` now documents that their signs are part of the name. The original exact M34-S2 examples are **FIXED**, but its general repair is incomplete for otherwise-ignored characters. A membership oracle which merely compares the output of the faulty `_sig` cannot detect this failure; the separate normalization-invariance and chemical-identity checks do.

The 70-case mutation matrix has 56 broken ignored-character equivalences: 14 negative-artemisinin cases draw its positive-rotation node, 14 negative-nicotine cases draw CHEMBL3, and 28 racemic-nicotine cases lose recall. Fourteen positive-nicotine controls remain undrawn. Only the artemisinin cohort is counted here as the independently established wrong-identity blocker; the nicotine controls are not extra blockers.

## Exact prior repairs

**M34-B1 FIXED.** `Cabozantinib (s)-malate`, `cabozantinibsmalate`, `CABOZANTINIB S-MALATE`, `Cabozantinib (s)-malate?`, `Cabozantinib/(s)-malate`, and `Cabozantinib\u200b(s)-malate` all refuse over HTTP. The preferred-name refusal is the explicitly recorded over-refusal, not a new blocker. [HTTP results](rotation_http.json).

**M34-S2 FIXED for its original exact inputs.** `(+)-NICOTINE` and `(+)NICOTINE` return `not_found`; `(-)-nicotine` returns `not_found`; `(+/-)NICOTINE`, `(±)-nicotine`, and `(+/−)NICOTINE` draw CHEMBL440464. M35-B1 above is a distinct incomplete-normalization reproduction. [HTTP results](rotation_http.json).

**M34-S1 FIXED for bracket recall.** `What trials evaluate (NICOTINE)?` and `What does [NICOTINE] target?` link CHEMBL3; the map returns `not_one_name`. `What trials evaluate (+/-)NICOTINE?` still has no entity and no map center. Added wrappers such as `What trials evaluate (+/-)(NICOTINE)?` link CHEMBL3 but remain undrawn on the map; the root/contract lane separately compares these new question-only regressions to the parent. [Direct controls](question_descriptor_brackets.json).

## Expanded source universe and refusal checks

`source_audit.py` reconstructs source membership from the dictionary/SQLite records, not `Engine.names`: every drug's preferred name and alias record, the entire lookup index, every node label, target symbols, company keys and disease keys. It also tests the canonical drug-index key strings as extra inputs. There are **34,429 source records / 22,956 distinct inputs**: **16,015 literal stored spellings/read keys**, plus **6,941 additional canonical drug-index key strings**. The literal universe is the prior 14,639 strings plus all 1,376 distinct omitted alias spellings. All source inputs are within the HTTP 500-character limit (maximum 231). [Source records](all_source_records.json), [runnable oracle](source_audit.py).

The sweep completed in **605.33 seconds**, exercising all **9,581 raw drug-alias records** as part of the source inventory, with **zero membership, silent-ambiguity, choice-set or stored-refusal failures and zero exceptions**. Overall results are 10,088 centers, 12,486 `alias_only`, 354 refusals, eight choices, five `not_found` and 15 `not_one_name`. The 16,015 literal-source cohort separately yields 9,081 centers, 6,738 `alias_only`, 187 refusals, six choices and three `not_found`. [Full summary](source_summary.json), [classified literal/key counts](source_classified_summary.json), [all exact results](source_sweep.json). These times overlap other audit jobs and are not an isolated performance benchmark.

The raw-alias list contains **1,696 records / 1,420 unique spellings missing from the same drug's lookup-index records**. Under the previous round's broader global-spelling definition, **1,652 records / 1,376 unique spellings** are absent from the previous literal source universe entirely. Both definitions include the same **18 omitted refused spellings**; all **29 source inputs sharing their signatures now refuse**. Actual drug preferred-name aliases and preferred-name index records all equal `drug['name']`; all live drug labels equal it too. Therefore skipping redundant preferred-name alias/index records does not leave an actual omitted alternate preferred spelling in this dataset. [Exact discrepancy/equality checks](source_discrepancies.json), [both omission definitions and refusal closure](omitted_refusal_summary.json).

The variants test covers **182 actual refused stored spellings × 13 forms = 2,366 inputs; all refuse**. This includes the 18 previously omitted refused spellings, not just the earlier indexed cohort. In addition, **84 refused canonical drug-index keys × 13 forms = 1,092 inputs** all remain undrawn: 1,074 refusals, 12 `alias_only`, six `not_found`. Those 18 non-refused terminal replies arise from three canonical strings which are not literal aliases; they are not silently counted as stored-name closure passes or wrong-center failures. [Classified summary](refusal_variants_classified.json), [complete mutations](refusal_variants.json).

Round 35 asks for every stored drug spelling in the refusal closure and for optical descriptors to remain significant. M34-B1's actual gap is closed: all full-alias source spellings are inspected, and the same-signature Cabozantinib preferred name now refuses as documented. The exact M34-S2 nicotine examples also pass, and ordinary brackets around the shared-key preferred name regain question recall.

**M35-B1 makes the new optical-sign guarantee incomplete.** `(-\u200b)-ARTEMISININ` draws CHEMBL269671 although `(-)-ARTEMISININ` remains undrawn. The new descriptor regex runs before invisibles are removed, so the negative sign is subsequently lost as punctuation. Source records and independent ChEBI/primary synthesis references establish the drawn molecule as (+)-artemisinin, distinct from the negative antipode. This is not a new data defect or a waiver-covered refused-preferred-name case. Unlike Round 34's broad punctuation limitation, it contradicts Round 35's explicit retained-sign rule composed with its existing ignored-invisible rule.

The expanded source and mutation suites, live ambiguity/retirement fixtures and significant-suffix probes provide bounded positives; their raw outputs and counts are linked above. They cannot cancel the separate optical counterexample. New free-text question wrapper regressions remain nonblocking under the declared scope. **M33-S1: FIXED for the original exact wrong-record reproductions.** `What trials evaluate (+/-)NICOTINE?` and `What does (+/-)NICOTINE target?` still abstain in both parent and HEAD, producing zero evidence in all three question APIs. Bare `(+/-)NICOTINE` still resolves to CHEMBL440464. This is closure by abstention, not restored racemic-name recall inside sentences.

**M35-S1 — brackets enclosing a name hide a neighboring rotation descriptor from the question guard.** This is a new **question-only should-fix**, outside the map's blocker scope. At `src/query.py:898-899`, the relaxed boundary checks only the character immediately beside the preferred name. An opening bracket before NICOTINE or closing bracket after it is now allowed even when the next outer text contains a descriptor. +## Code blocker
+
+### M35-B1 — an ignored invisible character defeats optical-sign preservation
+
+**Location:** `src/app.py:278-282`, `_sig`. The new regular expressions recognize optical descriptors before the final character filter removes invisible characters and combining marks. An inserted U+200B is not matched by `\s*`; consequently the ASCII minus remains punctuation and is deleted by that final filter. There is no later descriptor pass.
+
+The following are fresh HTTP results. In escaped spellings, `\u200b`, `\ufeff`, `\u2060`, and `\u0301` denote **actual single Unicode characters**, not six literal backslash characters. Complete inputs, responses, source records, and code-point captures are linked below.
+
+| Exact input / reproducible Python spelling | `_sig` | Map result |
+|---|---|---|
+| `ARTEMISININ` | `artemisinin` | center **CHEMBL269671** |
+| `(-)-ARTEMISININ` | `−artemisinin` (U+2212) | `not_one_name` |
+| `"(-\u200b)-ARTEMISININ"` | `artemisinin` | **center CHEMBL269671** |
+| `"(-\ufeff)-ARTEMISININ"` | `artemisinin` | **same center** |
+| `"(-\u2060)-ARTEMISININ"` | `artemisinin` | **same center** |
+| `"(-\u0301)-ARTEMISININ"` | `artemisinin` | **same center** |
+| `"(\u2212\u200b)-ARTEMISININ"` | `−artemisinin` | `not_one_name` |
+| `(+)-ARTEMISININ` | `+artemisinin` | `alias_only` |
+| `CHEMBL269671` | ID path | center CHEMBL269671, permitted ID control |
+
+[Independent root HTTP/source confirmation](root_optical_confirmation.json), [separate HTTP/code-point reproduction](attacks/rotation_http.json), [70-case descriptor mutation matrix](attacks/rotation_mutations.json), [source pointers and summary](attacks/rotation_source_and_summary.json). The matrix has **56 sign-normalization invariance failures and 14 wrong-sign ARTEMISININ draws**; these are variants of one defect, not 14 independent blockers.
+
+**Identity evidence:** `data/processed/resolved.json:1`, `dictionary.drugs.CHEMBL269671`, has preferred name `ARTEMISININ`, with `aliases[1].alias == "(+)-artemisinin"`; raw provenance is `data/raw/drugs.json:1`, `data[768].drug`, including its first ChEMBL synonym. The live node has the same preferred label. ChEBI identifies artemisinin as the positive optical form. A primary synthesis study explicitly distinguishes synthetic negative artemisinin from its natural positive antipode. [ChEBI](https://www.ebi.ac.uk/chebi/CHEBI:223316), [Krieger et al., 2018, PMID 29723442](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29723442/). The source record is consistent with those references; this is a **code normalization defect**, not a newly misfiled synonym.
+
+**Why this is blocking in this round:** the new repair explicitly makes the optical descriptor part of the name, while the retained comparison contract ignores invisible characters and accents. Those rules together require `(-)-ARTEMISININ` and `(-\u200b)-ARTEMISININ` to retain the same negative sign. The latter instead draws a different optical form. The exact original M34-S2 examples are fixed; that does not close this composition failure. Round 34 treated punctuation erasure as a nonblocking limitation of the then-prescribed comparison. This round explicitly changes that prescription to preserve optical descriptors. This finding therefore does not silently broaden the review to arbitrary chemical punctuation.
+
+Under the **buggy implementation** of `_sig`, the admitted input does equal the stored signature. An oracle that calls that same function consequently passes. The counterexample rests on the two documented transformation requirements and independently established identity, rather than a claimed failure of that circular membership test. It is not one of the recorded over-refusals, excluded brands/synonyms, tracer exceptions, or free-text question leaks.
+
+The repair needs to make ignored-character/diacritic normalization and optical-descriptor recognition compose correctly before punctuation is discarded, or fail closed when an optical descriptor cannot be interpreted safely. Verification should preserve `−`, `+`, and `±` through invisible/combining-character variants and must not enable additional stored-name admission. No production repair was made.
+

+## Question-linker regression, separate from the blocker
+
+### M35-S1 — wrapping the preferred name hides an adjacent stereochemical descriptor
+
+**Nonblocking question-only regression**, `src/query.py:898-899`. The new boundary expression permits an opening bracket immediately before the preferred-name token and a closing bracket immediately after it. It does not inspect past those brackets for a descriptor. Exact parent/current comparisons show:

+| Input | Parent query.py | HEAD query.py / HTTP |
+|---|---|---|
+| `What trials evaluate (+/-)(NICOTINE)?` | no entity/evidence | CHEMBL3 NICOTINE, two evidence rows |

+The bare racemic control `(+/-)NICOTINE` names CHEMBL440464. All three question APIs receive the erroneous CHEMBL3 evidence for these wrapped cases. The map returns `not_one_name`, so this does **not** independently fail the map approval bar. +# Independent relation-map review — round 35
+
+Reviewed **HEAD `56eb3d6297f2878d008e5edd9eb5eade62a0841c`**, parent `1f6017e8ae7923d21cfafca64b1285331e0d2c18`, on 2026-11-04. **CHANGES REQUIRED: one code blocker.** The exact M34-B1, S1 and S2 reproductions are fixed, but the optical-sign repair runs before removal of invisible characters. An invisible character inside `(-)` makes its minus disappear, admitting a negative optical isomer as an unqualified preferred-name node. A separate question-linker regression is nonblocking under the given scope. No new upstream data defect was established.

FIXED below closes an exact original reproduction under the present policy; it does not certify every related spelling, restore excluded brand recall, or imply upstream data curation. No tracked file was edited.

+## Code blocker
+
+### M35-B1 — an ignored invisible character defeats optical-sign preservation
+
+**Location:** `src/app.py:278-282`, `_sig`. The new regular expressions recognize optical descriptors before the final character filter removes invisible characters and combining marks. An inserted U+200B is not matched by `\s*`; consequently the ASCII minus remains punctuation and is deleted by that final filter. There is no later descriptor pass.
+
+The following are fresh HTTP results. In escaped spellings, `\u200b`, `\ufeff`, `\u2060`, and `\u0301` denote **actual single Unicode characters**, not six literal backslash characters. Complete inputs, responses, source records, and code-point captures are linked below.
+
+| Exact input / reproducible Python spelling | `_sig` | Map result |
+|---|---|---|
+| `ARTEMISININ` | `artemisinin` | center **CHEMBL269671** |
+| `(-)-ARTEMISININ` | `−artemisinin` (U+2212) | `not_one_name` |
+| `"(-\u200b)-ARTEMISININ"` | `artemisinin` | **center CHEMBL269671** |
+| `"(-\ufeff)-ARTEMISININ"` | `artemisinin` | **same center** |
+| `"(-\u2060)-ARTEMISININ"` | `artemisinin` | **same center** |
+| `"(-\u0301)-ARTEMISININ"` | `artemisinin` | **same center** |
+| `"(\u2212\u200b)-ARTEMISININ"` | `−artemisinin` | `not_one_name` |
+| `(+)-ARTEMISININ` | `+artemisinin` | `alias_only` |
+| `CHEMBL269671` | ID path | center CHEMBL269671, permitted ID control |
+
+[Independent root HTTP/source confirmation](root_optical_confirmation.json), [separate HTTP/code-point reproduction](attacks/rotation_http.json), [70-case descriptor mutation matrix](attacks/rotation_mutations.json), [source pointers and summary](attacks/rotation_source_and_summary.json). The matrix has **56 sign-normalization invariance failures and 14 wrong-sign ARTEMISININ draws**; these are variants of one defect, not 14 independent blockers.
+
+**Identity evidence:** `data/processed/resolved.json:1`, `dictionary.drugs.CHEMBL269671`, has preferred name `ARTEMISININ`, with `aliases[1].alias == "(+)-artemisinin"`; raw provenance is `data/raw/drugs.json:1`, `data[768].drug`, including its first ChEMBL synonym. The live node has the same preferred label. ChEBI identifies artemisinin as the positive optical form. A primary synthesis study explicitly distinguishes synthetic negative artemisinin from its natural positive antipode. [ChEBI](https://www.ebi.ac.uk/chebi/CHEBI:223316), [Krieger et al., 2018, PMID 29723442](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29723442/). The source record is consistent with those references; this is a **code normalization defect**, not a newly misfiled synonym.
+
+**Why this is blocking in this round:** the new repair explicitly makes the optical descriptor part of the name, while the retained comparison contract ignores invisible characters and accents. Those rules together require `(-)-ARTEMISININ` and `(-\u200b)-ARTEMISININ` to retain the same negative sign. The latter instead draws a different optical form. The exact original M34-S2 examples are fixed; that does not close this composition failure. Round 34 treated punctuation erasure as a nonblocking limitation of the then-prescribed comparison. This round explicitly changes that prescription to preserve optical descriptors. This finding therefore does not silently broaden the review to arbitrary chemical punctuation.
+
+Under the **buggy implementation** of `_sig`, the admitted input does equal the stored signature. A purely signature-membership test consequently passes, including an independently implemented oracle that repeats the same normalization order. The counterexample rests on the two documented transformation requirements and independently established identity, rather than a claimed failure of that narrower membership test. It is not one of the recorded over-refusals, excluded brands/synonyms, tracer exceptions, or free-text question leaks.
+
+The repair needs to make ignored-character/diacritic normalization and optical-descriptor recognition compose correctly before punctuation is discarded, or fail closed when an optical descriptor cannot be interpreted safely. Verification should preserve `−`, `+`, and `±` through invisible/combining-character variants and must not enable additional stored-name admission. No production repair was made.
+

+## Question-linker regression, separate from the blocker
+
+### M35-S1 — wrapping the preferred name hides an adjacent stereochemical descriptor
+
+**Nonblocking question-only regression**, `src/query.py:898-899`. The new boundary expression permits an opening bracket immediately before the preferred-name token and a closing bracket immediately after it. It does not inspect past those brackets for a descriptor. Exact parent/current comparisons show:
+
+| Input | Parent query.py | HEAD query.py / HTTP |
+|---|---|---|
+| `What trials evaluate (+/-)(NICOTINE)?` | no entity/evidence | CHEMBL3 NICOTINE, two evidence rows |
+| `What does (+/-)[NICOTINE] target?` | no entity/evidence | CHEMBL3 NICOTINE, two evidence rows |
+| `What trials evaluate (NICOTINE)(+/-)?` | no entity/evidence | CHEMBL3 NICOTINE, two evidence rows |
+| `What trials evaluate [NICOTINE](+/-)?` | no entity/evidence | CHEMBL3 NICOTINE, two evidence rows |
+| `What trials evaluate (NICOTINE)(+)?` | no entity/evidence | CHEMBL3 NICOTINE, two evidence rows |
+
+The bare racemic control `(+/-)NICOTINE` names CHEMBL440464. All three question APIs receive the erroneous CHEMBL3 evidence for these wrapped cases. The map returns `not_one_name`, so this does **not** independently fail the map approval bar. [Expanded parent/current comparison](contract/expanded_question_comparison.json), [summary and HTTP cases](contract/expanded_question_summary.json), [root corroboration](root_exact_http.json).
+
+The expanded comparison covers **six shared preferred names × 26 formats × two question wrappers = 312 cases**, with 110 changed readings (a mixture of intended recall recoveries and descriptor regressions), **zero map centers**. The inherited **99-key / 708-case** comparison and all **36 shipped demos** are unchanged. Therefore the repair note's corpus result is reproduced, but that older corpus misses the new bracket-descriptor composition. [Shared-key corpus](contract/shared_key_question_comparison.json), [demos](contract/demo_previous_comparison.json). The separate 47-product/141-question refusal matrix retains its expected refusals. [Product controls](contract/product_questions.json).

+## Expanded source and attack coverage
+
+The oracle reconstructs admitted membership from dictionary and SQL records, **not `Engine.names`**. It includes every drug preferred name and alias, every index entry, node label, target symbol, company key and disease key. It additionally probes canonical drug-index keys as inputs. The **22,956 inputs** comprise **16,015 literal stored spellings/read keys** (the prior 14,639 plus 1,376 globally omitted raw-alias spellings) and **6,941 additional canonical drug-index key strings**. All are within the HTTP length limit; maximum length is 231. The complete sweep uses direct `Engine.explore`; selected cases are separately confirmed through HTTP. [Source inventory](attacks/all_source_records.json), [independent oracle](attacks/source_audit.py), [classified counts](attacks/source_classified_summary.json).
+
+Overall results: **10,088 centers, 12,486 `alias_only`, 354 refused, eight choices, five `not_found`, 15 `not_one_name`**, zero failures/exceptions. The literal-source subset yields 9,081 centers, 6,738 `alias_only`, 187 refused, six choices and three `not_found`. Raw-alias spelling occurrence counts and unique-string counts are kept separate. Same-drug index omissions are **1,696 records / 1,420 spellings**; the prior round's global-spelling omission definition yields **1,652 records / 1,376 spellings**. Both contain the same **18 omitted refused spellings**; all **29 source inputs sharing their signatures now refuse**. All stored preferred-name alias/index spellings and live drug labels agree with `drug['name']`; skipping redundant preferred-name entries leaves no actual alternate-spelling gap in this dataset. [Omission closure](attacks/omitted_refusal_summary.json), [source discrepancies/equality](attacks/source_discrepancies.json).
+
+The refusal sweep exercises **182 actual refused stored spellings × 13 forms = 2,366 inputs; all refuse**. A separate **84 canonical keys × 13 = 1,092** probes all remain undrawn: 1,074 refused, 12 `alias_only`, six `not_found`. The latter 18 are terminal-state differences on three synthetic canonical keys, not failures to refuse literal stored names and not wrong-center draws. This distinction corrects the broader variable name `refused_stored_names: 266` in the unclassified summary. [Classified refusal results](attacks/refusal_variants_classified.json), [full matrix](attacks/refusal_variants.json).
+
+**1,066 preferred names × six significant suffixes = 6,396 inputs** draw no center. **54 private retirement cases / 10 assertion groups** pass: both live → choose, one retired → remaining live candidate, all retired → not-found, including lowercase IDs and no retired-Roche fallback to a drug alias. Production DB hash remains unchanged. [Suffixes](attacks/significant_suffixes_summary.json), [retirement results](attacks/retired_private_fixture.json). These successes do not cover the separate optical-invisible mutation invariant.

+
+Reviewed **HEAD `56eb3d6297f2878d008e5edd9eb5eade62a0841c`**, parent `1f6017e8ae7923d21cfafca64b1285331e0d2c18`, on 2026-11-04. **CHANGES REQUIRED: one code blocker.** The exact M34-B1, S1 and S2 reproductions are fixed, but the optical-sign repair runs before removal of invisible characters. An invisible character inside `(-)` makes its minus disappear, admitting a negative optical isomer as an unqualified preferred-name node. A separate question-linker regression is nonblocking under the given scope. No new upstream data defect was established.

FIXED below closes an exact original reproduction under the present policy; it does not certify every related spelling, restore excluded brand recall, or imply upstream data curation. No tracked file was edited.

+## Code blocker
+
+### M35-B1 — an ignored invisible character defeats optical-sign preservation
+
+**Location:** `src/app.py:278-282`, `_sig`. The new regular expressions recognize optical descriptors before the final character filter removes invisible characters and combining marks. An inserted U+200B is not matched by `\s*`; consequently the ASCII minus remains punctuation and is deleted by that final filter. There is no later descriptor pass.
+
+The following are fresh HTTP results. In escaped spellings, `\u200b`, `\ufeff`, `\u2060`, and `\u0301` denote **actual single Unicode characters**, not six literal backslash characters. Complete inputs, responses, source records, and code-point captures are linked below.
+
+| Exact input / reproducible Python spelling | `_sig` | Map result |
+|---|---|---|
+| `ARTEMISININ` | `artemisinin` | center **CHEMBL269671** |
+| `(-)-ARTEMISININ` | `−artemisinin` (U+2212) | `not_one_name` |
+| `"(-\u200b)-ARTEMISININ"` | `artemisinin` | **center CHEMBL269671** |
+| `"(-\ufeff)-ARTEMISININ"` | `artemisinin` | **same center** |
+| `"(-\u2060)-ARTEMISININ"` | `artemisinin` | **same center** |
+| `"(-\u0301)-ARTEMISININ"` | `artemisinin` | **same center** |
+| `"(\u2212\u200b)-ARTEMISININ"` | `−artemisinin` | `not_one_name` |
+| `(+)-ARTEMISININ` | `+artemisinin` | `alias_only` |
+| `CHEMBL269671` | ID path | center CHEMBL269671, permitted ID control |
+
+[Independent root HTTP/source confirmation](root_optical_confirmation.json), [separate HTTP/code-point reproduction](attacks/rotation_http.json), [70-case descriptor mutation matrix](attacks/rotation_mutations.json), [source pointers and summary](attacks/rotation_source_and_summary.json). The matrix has **56 sign-normalization invariance failures and 14 wrong-sign ARTEMISININ draws**; these are variants of one defect, not 14 independent blockers.
+
+**Identity evidence:** `data/processed/resolved.json:1`, `dictionary.drugs.CHEMBL269671`, has preferred name `ARTEMISININ`, with `aliases[1].alias == "(+)-artemisinin"`; raw provenance is `data/raw/drugs.json:1`, `data[768].drug`, including its first ChEMBL synonym. The live node has the same preferred label. ChEBI identifies artemisinin as the positive optical form. A primary synthesis study explicitly distinguishes synthetic negative artemisinin from its natural positive antipode. [ChEBI](https://www.ebi.ac.uk/chebi/CHEBI:223316), [Krieger et al., 2018, PMID 29723442](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29723442/). The source record is consistent with those references; this is a **code normalization defect**, not a newly misfiled synonym.
+
+**Why this is blocking in this round:** the new repair explicitly makes the optical descriptor part of the name, while the retained comparison contract ignores invisible characters and accents. Those rules together require `(-)-ARTEMISININ` and `(-\u200b)-ARTEMISININ` to retain the same negative sign. The latter instead draws a different optical form. The exact original M34-S2 examples are fixed; that does not close this composition failure. Round 34 treated punctuation erasure as a nonblocking limitation of the then-prescribed comparison. This round explicitly changes that prescription to preserve optical descriptors. This finding therefore does not silently broaden the review to arbitrary chemical punctuation.
+
+Under the **buggy implementation** of `_sig`, the admitted input does equal the stored signature. A purely signature-membership test consequently passes, including an independently implemented oracle that repeats the same normalization order. The counterexample rests on the two documented transformation requirements and independently established identity, rather than a claimed failure of that narrower membership test. It is not one of the recorded over-refusals, excluded brands/synonyms, tracer exceptions, or free-text question leaks.
+
+The repair needs to make ignored-character/diacritic normalization and optical-descriptor recognition compose correctly before punctuation is discarded, or fail closed when an optical descriptor cannot be interpreted safely. Verification should preserve `−`, `+`, and `±` through invisible/combining-character variants and must not enable additional stored-name admission. No production repair was made.
+

+## Question-linker regression, separate from the blocker
+
+### M35-S1 — wrapping the preferred name hides an adjacent stereochemical descriptor
+
+**Nonblocking question-only regression**, `src/query.py:898-899`. The new boundary expression permits an opening bracket immediately before the preferred-name token and a closing bracket immediately after it. It does not inspect past those brackets for a descriptor. Exact parent/current comparisons show:
+
+| Input | Parent query.py | HEAD query.py / HTTP |
+|---|---|---|
+| `What trials evaluate (+/-)(NICOTINE)?` | no entity/evidence | CHEMBL3 NICOTINE, two evidence rows |
+| `What does (+/-)[NICOTINE] target?` | no entity/evidence | CHEMBL3 NICOTINE, two evidence rows |
+| `What trials evaluate (NICOTINE)(+/-)?` | no entity/evidence | CHEMBL3 NICOTINE, two evidence rows |
+| `What trials evaluate [NICOTINE](+/-)?` | no entity/evidence | CHEMBL3 NICOTINE, two evidence rows |
+| `What trials evaluate (NICOTINE)(+)?` | no entity/evidence | CHEMBL3 NICOTINE, two evidence rows |
+
+The bare racemic control `(+/-)NICOTINE` names CHEMBL440464. All three question APIs receive the erroneous CHEMBL3 evidence for these wrapped cases. The map returns `not_one_name`, so this does **not** independently fail the map approval bar. [Expanded parent/current comparison](contract/expanded_question_comparison.json), [summary and HTTP cases](contract/expanded_question_summary.json), [root corroboration](root_exact_http.json).
+
+The expanded comparison covers **six shared preferred names × 26 formats × two question wrappers = 312 cases**, with 110 changed readings (a mixture of intended recall recoveries and descriptor regressions), **zero map centers**. The inherited **99-key / 708-case** comparison and all **36 shipped demos** are unchanged. Therefore the repair note's corpus result is reproduced, but that older corpus misses the new bracket-descriptor composition. +## Expanded source and attack coverage
+
+The oracle reconstructs admitted membership from dictionary and SQL records, **not `Engine.names`**. It includes every drug preferred name and alias, every index entry, node label, target symbol, company key and disease key. It additionally probes canonical drug-index keys as inputs. The **22,956 inputs** comprise **16,015 literal stored spellings/read keys** (the prior 14,639 plus 1,376 globally omitted raw-alias spellings) and **6,941 additional canonical drug-index key strings**. All are within the HTTP length limit; maximum length is 231. The complete sweep uses direct `Engine.explore`; selected cases are separately confirmed through HTTP. [Source inventory](attacks/all_source_records.json), [independent oracle](attacks/source_audit.py), [classified counts](attacks/source_classified_summary.json).
+
+Overall results: **10,088 centers, 12,486 `alias_only`, 354 refused, eight choices, five `not_found`, 15 `not_one_name`**, zero failures/exceptions. The literal-source subset yields 9,081 centers, 6,738 `alias_only`, 187 refused, six choices and three `not_found`. Raw-alias spelling occurrence counts and unique-string counts are kept separate. Same-drug index omissions are **1,696 records / 1,420 spellings**; the prior round's global-spelling omission definition yields **1,652 records / 1,376 spellings**. Both contain the same **18 omitted refused spellings**; all **29 source inputs sharing their signatures now refuse**. All stored preferred-name alias/index spellings and live drug labels agree with `drug['name']`; skipping redundant preferred-name entries leaves no actual alternate-spelling gap in this dataset. [Omission closure](attacks/omitted_refusal_summary.json), [source discrepancies/equality](attacks/source_discrepancies.json).
+
+The refusal sweep exercises **182 actual refused stored spellings × 13 forms = 2,366 inputs; all refuse**. A separate **84 canonical keys × 13 = 1,092** probes all remain undrawn: 1,074 refused, 12 `alias_only`, six `not_found`. The latter 18 are terminal-state differences on three synthetic canonical keys, not failures to refuse literal stored names and not wrong-center draws. This distinction corrects the broader variable name `refused_stored_names: 266` in the unclassified summary. [Classified refusal results](attacks/refusal_variants_classified.json), [full matrix](attacks/refusal_variants.json).
+
+**1,066 preferred names × six significant suffixes = 6,396 inputs** draw no center. **54 private retirement cases / 10 assertion groups** pass: both live → choose, one retired → remaining live candidate, all retired → not-found, including lowercase IDs and no retired-Roche fallback to a drug alias. Production DB hash remains unchanged. [Suffixes](attacks/significant_suffixes_summary.json), [retirement results](attacks/retired_private_fixture.json). These successes do not cover the separate optical-invisible mutation invariant.

The ledgers overlap and are not added as unique-finding counts.

The original **7,511-key alias sweep** and two **1,648-question frozen/historical replays** completed; each of the latter reports 1,648 identical and zero errors. The separate no-relink adapter reports 72 identical, 1,552 entity-metadata-only and 24 semantic differences against its constructed old baseline; those are not HEAD-versus-parent counts. The unavailable original M22 generator is **not** claimed to have run: its byte-identical saved-input adapter reran the recorded **58 inputs**. Old assertions that expected a longer input to draw are not substituted for the current admission oracle. Inherited residuals remain for 400 distinct `isa` pieces, 4,000 U+FDFA characters, 1,000 punctuation-separated `x` tokens, 1,000 `isa` matches and joined-item rescans. The current cold request / queued-map times are respectively **7.032 / 6.980 s**, **8.690 / 8.632 s**, **0.526 s direct median** for punctuation tokens, **8.234 / 8.178 s**, and **7.990 / 7.952 s**. These are successful completions with residual overhead, not new timeouts or proof of a commit-caused performance regression; audit jobs ran concurrently. All seven cost-server lifecycles retain DB hashes and close their ports. The bracket-descriptor question regression (M35-S1) is separately nonblocking. No additional source data defect was established. [Independent Spec review](attacks/findings.md#spec-axis-under-400-words).

+## Integrity and limits
+
+All **7,835 tracked files plus the processed database and resolved dictionary** match the root's pre-test SHA-256 baseline: **7,837 files checked, zero changed or added**, tracked git status clean, HEAD unchanged. Root stopped its owned CLI session on port 8935 after all requests finished; all **31 owned/tested ports** are closed. [Initial hashes](integrity_before.json), [final integrity](integrity_final.json), [server/port lifecycle](server_lifecycle.json). Scratch artifacts and this report are confined to m35. No production repair, source-data curation, external publication or model invocation was performed.
+
+This review establishes the exact counterexamples and bounded positive checks above. It does not certify exhaustive scientific identity for every source name, universal free-text refusal, graph-construction brand assignments, or rendered-browser layout. The new code blocker remains despite passing unit tests and full-source membership checks because those checks do not establish the intended composition of optical-sign and invisible-character rules.
